{"parentUuid":"7774f11e-06c1-4ad2-a981-c905617dd5d3","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"ac25c34de25263649","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- artifact-planner: Produce a self-contained, navigable HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPLIT STORYBOARD: each scene pairs a rich UI/mockup (left) with a before→after Mermaid diagram of the structural change (right), plus a \"what this lets you do\" line and an \"under the hood\" strip. Embeds a machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, walk through, or visualize any multi-step flow: onboarding, checkout, auth, OAuth integrations, wizards, subscription cancellation, push notifications, support ticket lifecycles, AI chat execution, CI/CD pipelines, refund flows, admin features, migrations, webhook lifecycles, or anything else with sequenced stages and decision points. Trigger on \"create an artifact for…\", \"plan this flow\", \"make a flow plan\", \"visualize this flow\", \"show me the stages of…\", \"mock up the flow for…\", \"I want to plan X visually\", \"build a walkthrough of…\", or any time the user has just described a feature/flow and would benefit from a single-page visual + structured spec they can revisit, share, or drop back into a new chat. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- artifact-planner-canvas: Produce a self-contained HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPATIAL CANVAS: the WHOLE plan on one pannable/zoomable Figma-style board — frontend mockups in a top band, each stage's before→after Mermaid diagram directly beneath (dashed spine connectors), branches as tinted lanes with fork/rejoin curves, decisions and open questions as sticky notes placed next to the stages they affect, and double-click-anywhere comment stickies. This is the CANVAS variant of `artifact-planner` (which pages one scene at a time as a split storyboard): prefer the canvas when the user wants to see everything at once, compare stages spatially, or leave free-floating comments; prefer the paged storyboard for linear walkthroughs and presentations. Embeds the same machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, or visualize any multi-step flow (onboarding, checkout, auth, wizards, CI/CD pipelines, migrations, webhook lifecycles…) AND signals a canvas: \"canvas artifact\", \"spatial plan\", \"put the plan on a canvas/board\", \"Figma-style board\", \"show me the whole flow at once\", \"map it out spatially\" — or when they ask for the canvas version of an existing artifact-plan. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- auto-rough-cut: Turn a folder of raw class-video recordings — separately recorded pristine voice tracks (Audacity .wav) plus their screen/camera videos (.mp4) — into a draggable Adobe Premiere Pro FCP7 XML that is silence-cut to Ray's Recut preferences, has the pristine audio synced and linked to the video, transcribes every kept chunk, and flags repeated takes / filler / breaths in PURPLE for a fast review pass. Use this skill whenever Ray points at a folder of recording pairs and wants a rough cut, first assembly, or \"silence cut\", or says things like \"cut the silences\", \"sync my audio and video and remove the dead air\", \"make a Premiere XML from these recordings\", \"apply my Recut process / record preferences\", \"flag the retakes\", \"auto rough-cut this video\", \"turn my Audacity audio + screen recording into a timeline\", or stages new recordings under a \"[Class Videos]\" / to-film folder. Trigger it even if Recut, transcription, or retakes aren't all named — any \"remove the silence and give me a Premiere XML I can still drag around\" request for paired audio+video is this skill. Distinct from recut-xml-fixer (which only repairs an XML Recut already exported); this skill does the whole cut from raw media.\n- auto-spec: Turn a feature brief (inline text or a stub plan file) into an implementation-ready plan via an autonomous two-agent interview, an external-API research verification pass, and a two-agent codebase-grounded planning pass — and, in its bootstrap mode, build/refresh the personalized digital twin that powers that interview by mining your Claude Code conversation history. Use when the user says \"auto spec\", \"spec autopilot\", \"auto interview\", \"flesh out this plan\", \"/auto-spec <file or brief>\", or otherwise wants a plan written without manually answering questions; ALSO use for \"create my auto-spec\", \"clone my brain\", \"make a spec twin\", \"personalize auto-spec\", \"build my decision profile\", or any request to analyze conversation history for decision patterns. Produces a single plan file with Feature Brief, Spec Decisions, External Research, Codebase Map, and phased Implementation Plan — ready to hand to `/implement-task` — or, in bootstrap mode, a refreshed `decision_profile.md` + `qa_examples.json` digital twin.\n- chronicle\n- codex-consult: OpenAI Codex CLI wrapper — three modes. Code review: independent diff review via\ncodex review with pass/fail gate. Challenge: adversarial mode that tries to break\nyour code. Consult: ask codex anything with session continuity for follow-ups.\nThe \"200 IQ autistic developer\" second opinion. Use when asked to \"codex review\",\n\"codex challenge\", \"ask codex\", \"second opinion\", or \"consult codex\".\n- improve-claude-md: improve a CLAUDE.md file using <important if> blocks to improve instruction adherence\n- interview: Relentlessly interrogate a plan, decision, or idea one question at a time until the user and Claude reach a genuinely shared understanding. Use this whenever the user wants to stress-test their thinking, pressure-test a plan, be challenged on an idea, or says any of \"interview me\", \"grill me\", \"grill this\", \"poke holes in this\", \"stress-test this\", \"ask me everything\", \"interrogate me\", \"what am I missing\", or \"/interview\". Also reach for it when the user drops a half-formed plan and asks for questions before you build anything, or when a request is underspecified enough that guessing would waste real work. Do not write code, files, or plans while this skill is active until the user says the picture is complete.\n- slackbot-message: Send Slack messages to a named channel via a bot user. Use this skill whenever you need to notify Ray on Slack, post a status update, alert a channel, deliver a short summary, or when he asks you to \"post to slack\", \"message #channel\", \"send a slack message\", \"ping slack\", \"slack me\", \"let me know on slack\", or names a specific Slack channel by name. Also use it after long-running tasks finish if Ray asked for Slack notifications. The bot is invited to the relevant channels, so the script can post to any channel by its human-readable name (no need to know channel IDs).\n- spec-developer-v2: Interview the user about a rough idea or brief with sharp, non-obvious product and technical questions, then write a concise implementation-ready spec. Use when the user wants to spec something out, develop a vague idea into requirements, or turn a product concept into a detailed plan.\n- sync-skills: Commit and push GLOBAL skill changes to the personal skills git repo\n(github.com/ray-amjad/private-skills). Auto-detects what changed in\n~/.claude/skills/, stages the changes, generates a meaningful commit message,\nand pushes to origin main. Use this skill whenever you finish creating or\nediting a global skill, or when the user says \"sync skills\", \"push skills\",\n\"save skills\", \"/sync-skills\", or asks to commit skill changes to git.\nIMPORTANT: Only trigger this skill when working at the project level (i.e., when\nthe working directory is a project repo, not from within ~/.claude/skills/ itself).\nDo NOT use this for project-scoped skills (e.g. skills under a project's own\n.claude/skills/, like ray-os's sentence-mining) — those are committed to that\nproject's repo, not here.\n- ultragoal\n- vercel-logs: Check Vercel production logs, errors, and build output. Use this skill whenever you need to\ndiagnose a deployment error, check for runtime errors, investigate 500s, or the user asks\n\"check the logs\", \"what's the error\", \"why is it broken\", \"check vercel logs\", or any\nrequest to look at Vercel deployment/runtime/build logs. Also trigger on `/vercel-logs`.\n\nCRITICAL: Also invoke this skill BEFORE running any `vercel logs` command yourself —\neven when no one asked you to. The CLI has non-obvious flag interactions (implicit\nfollow mode, stderr-only output, --since rejection) that cause hangs or silent failures\nif you guess at flags. If you're about to type `vercel logs` in a Bash call, load this\nskill first.\n- vercel-monitor: Watch a Vercel deployment in the background and notify when it reaches Ready/Error/Canceled.\nUse this skill whenever the user pushes to main, runs `git push`, deploys to Vercel, or asks\nto \"monitor the deploy\", \"watch the build\", \"check if it deployed\", or anything similar —\neven if they don't explicitly say \"Vercel\". Also trigger on `/vercel-monitor`. This replaces\nolder patterns of spawning a haiku subagent or polling via the Vercel MCP, both of which\nwaste tokens and context on large projects.\n- anki-cleanup\n- binary-explorer: Reverse-engineer any desktop application OR Claude Code's own binary to understand how it works behind the scenes — the APIs it calls, the internal flow, the models/providers it uses, its feature flags, config constants, embedded prompts, IPC protocols. For general apps it works on Electron apps (Wispr Flow, Slack, Discord, VS Code, Cursor, Notion, Linear…), native macOS .app bundles, single-executable bundles (Bun/Node/Deno/Go SEA), and raw Mach-O / ELF / PE binaries. It ALSO reverse-engineers Claude Code's own binary — it knows Claude Code's mangled variable conventions, feature-flag patterns, and embedded-prompt layout. Use this skill whenever the user points at a desktop app and wants to know \"how does X work under the hood\", \"what API does it call\", \"how does it do Y\", \"what's the flow for Z\", \"reverse engineer this app\", \"figure out how this feature works\", \"what endpoints does it hit\", \"does it run locally or in the cloud\", \"what model does it use\", or names a specific app path (.app, .exe, a binary). ALSO use it whenever the user asks about a Claude Code feature, feature flag, hidden toggle, internal prompt, or wants to understand how something in Claude Code works under the hood — especially unreleased, gated, or undocumented functionality — or says \"how does X work in the binary\", \"what does this feature flag do\", \"find the prompt for X\", \"reverse engineer X\", or mentions a specific codename/flag. Also trigger when the user wants to audit an app's network behavior, understand its architecture, or ext…\n- class-scriptwriter: Turn a raw idea, source tweet, video transcript, feature announcement, or rough outline into a polished video script for Ray's Agentic Coding School classes. Use this skill whenever the user says \"write a class script\", \"turn this into a class video\", \"script this for the [business/claude-code/prompt-engineering/skills/context-engineering/techniques/claude-chat/claude-cowork/correction] class\", \"draft an agentic coding school video\", or whenever the user is staging content in `/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os/projects/agentic-coding-school/to-film/` and wants it written up. Default to this skill for any new file destined for the `to-film/` tree, even if the user does not name it explicitly. Produces an essay-style markdown script with the correct frontmatter, horizontal rules between sections, inline image embed placeholders, and a Demo section, written in Ray's direct second-person voice.\n- content-cascade\n- discord-archive\n- excalidraw-bundle: Bundle a set of section images into a single .excalidraw file laid out as a grid — one column per section in document order, the section's variations stacked vertically — and save it to ~/Downloads, ready to drag into excalidraw.com or the Obsidian Excalidraw plugin.\n- excalidraw-codex: Generate excalidraw-style explanation images using the Codex CLI (codex exec --json) instead of a paid API. Use when the user wants visual explanations, diagrams, or illustrations in the hand-drawn excalidraw aesthetic and asks for the codex path. Codex's bundled image_gen tool runs through the logged-in account, so no GEMINI_API_KEY is required and it's often faster than the Gemini route. Triggers on \"generate an excalidraw image\", \"excalidraw-codex\", \"use codex for excalidraw\", or any request for excalidraw-style diagrams where no engine is named. This is the RECOMMENDED DEFAULT path for excalidraw image generation: it needs no API key and runs through the logged-in Codex account. Sibling skill [[excalidraw-gemini]] does the same job through the paid Gemini API. Default to codex whenever the user does not explicitly ask for the Gemini route.\n- excalidraw-codex-simple\n- excalidraw-deck: Build hand-drawn, Excalidraw-style HTML slide decks that explain a concept through sketch-aesthetic vector diagrams — rough.js strokes on a dark canvas, full-bleed, one diagram per slide with a caption underneath, arrow-key and tap navigation. Use this skill whenever the user wants explainer slides, a concept deck, diagram slides, or a talk/video presentation in the hand-drawn sketch look, or says things like \"make slides for X\", \"build a deck for X\", \"add a slide in this style\", \"turn this into a sketch/excalidraw-style deck\", \"another slide like the workflow deck\", or points at an existing rough.js deck and wants more in the same style. This is for self-contained vector-diagram decks — NOT the Claude Code terminal-aesthetic slides (use cc-terminal-slides) and NOT raster image generation (use excalidraw-gen, which calls an image model). Reach for this skill proactively for any \"explain this concept on slides\" request, even when the user does not say the word \"excalidraw\".\n- extract-wisdom: Extract structured wisdom, insights, and actionable takeaways from any content — podcasts, articles, videos, transcripts, books, or conversations. Use this skill whenever the user asks to \"extract wisdom\", \"summarize insights\", \"pull out key ideas\", \"what are the takeaways\", \"extract the best parts\", \"distill this content\", \"what did I learn from this\", or provides a transcript/article and asks for a structured breakdown. Also trigger when the user pastes or references long-form content and wants the signal extracted from the noise — even if they don't use the word \"wisdom\" specifically. Inspired by Daniel Miessler's Fabric pattern. Has a BATCH MODE for running this extraction across a whole YouTube channel in bulk: lists videos with yt-dlp, filters by duration, downloads transcripts, then fans out one extract-wisdom subagent per video in batches of 5, writing one wisdom file per video to disk. Trigger batch mode whenever the user wants wisdom/insights/takeaways from a YouTube channel in bulk: \"extract wisdom from this channel\", \"go through @<handle>'s long videos\", \"summarise everything on <channel>\", \"give me the best ideas from <creator>'s podcast\", \"process a whole channel into wisdom notes\", or \"I want to read the highlights from every video on @<handle> longer than 20 minutes\".\n- humanizer\n- idea-foundry\n- image-cleanup: Clean up unreferenced images in Obsidian vault folders. Deletes unused images, renames excalidraw files with descriptive names based on image content (using vision), and moves everything to vault root images/ folder. Use after picking favorites from excalidraw-gen batches. Triggers on \"clean up images\", \"delete unused images\", \"tidy up images\", \"image cleanup\", or \"/image-cleanup\".\n- linkedin: All LinkedIn tasks — writing posts (text or carousel), scouting sources to post about, checking post performance, browsing the feed, or analyzing competitors. Triggers on any mention of LinkedIn, including \"write a LinkedIn post\", \"make a LinkedIn carousel\", \"swipeable LinkedIn post\", \"LinkedIn slides\", \"check my LinkedIn\", \"how did my posts do\", \"LinkedIn competitors\", \"post on LinkedIn\", \"browse LinkedIn feed\". Also triggers on \"check post engagement\", \"update post metrics\", or reviewing social media performance. Also use it for finding something to post about — \"find me a LinkedIn topic\", \"run a source sweep\", \"scout me something on MCP\", \"what should I post today\", or reviewing the drafts the daily scout left in Slack\n- newsletter-writer: Write personal newsletters in a conversational, essay-style format inspired by Ali Abdaal's style. Use when the user wants to write a newsletter issue, draft an email to their list, write a LifeNotes-style essay, or asks for help with newsletter writing. Triggers on requests like \"write a newsletter\", \"draft a newsletter about...\", \"help me write my weekly email\", \"newsletter style\", or when the user provides a topic and wants it turned into a newsletter format.\n- rakuten-hotels\n- recut-xml-fixer: Reshape an FCP7 XML exported from a silence-cutting app (Recut, AutoEdit, Vika, etc.) so Adobe Premiere Pro imports it cleanly with the user's pristine external audio (e.g. Audio.wav from Audacity) linked to the video instead of the camera/screen-recording's own audio. Use this skill whenever the user points at an .xml or sequence file from Recut, AutoEdit, or any auto-cutter and wants to swap in their own audio track, drop the redundant on-camera audio, fix vertical alignment between video and audio cuts, or fix a Premiere \"import failed\" error on a Recut-style XML. Triggers on phrases like \"fix this Recut XML\", \"swap the audio in this premiere XML\", \"align the cuts to my Audio.wav\", \"remove the loom audio track\", \"merge V1 and A1 in this XML so they're linked\", or any reference to a workflow combining a silence-cutter app's XML with a separately recorded pristine audio file.\n- sentence-mining: Build and maintain Japanese sentence-mining cards for Anki, fully self-contained (no AnkiMorphs install required) and configurable per user via a one-time `/sentence-mining setup`. Six modes, and the skill ASKS which one you want on every launch. (1) Video mode — paste any Instagram reel, YouTube video/Short, TikTok, Twitter video, or local file → yt-dlp + AssemblyAI + SudachiPy + a built-in i+1 known-word diff produces draft cards. (2) Bank mode — give a list of target words → find the best example sentence for each through one canonical cascade (Immersion Kit → Nadeshiko → sentencesearch → kotu → your locally-indexed subs2srs .apkg banks), re-ranked by your own i+1, with native audio and a screenshot where the source ships one. (3) Replace mode — fix existing cards whose example sentence is bad (too short, a fragment, incomprehensible): pull a better sentence through the same cascade, edit the card in place (archiving the old sentence to a previous_versions field), and rehabilitate the card so you re-learn it fresh. (4) Leech mode — work the cards Anki flagged as leeches, but filtered by whether they are STILL failing: `tag:leech` is a scar Anki never removes, so most of a leech list is usually cards that recovered months ago (43 of Ray's 78 sat at 21-338 day intervals). Cards below the interval threshold get a fresh sentence through the cascade plus full rehabilitation (reps/lapses zeroed, due today); recovered cards just have the tag stripped with their scheduling untouched; words rarer than a JPDB frequen…\n- transcribe-youtube-video: Transcribe audio or video into a clean .txt + timestamped .srt + YouTube chapter timestamps. Re-encodes any WAV/MP4 to a small MP3 before upload, mines on-screen text with OCR to seed AssemblyAI key terms, runs AssemblyAI Universal-3 Pro speech-to-text, then generates punchy chapter markers from the SRT. Use whenever the user wants to transcribe a video or audio file, generate subtitles/captions or an SRT, create a YouTube transcript or chapters, or convert a recording to text. Also trigger when the user has a WAV/MP4/MOV and wants text out of it, even without saying 'transcribe'.\n- ungate-claude-code-feature: Enable a gated, experimental, or staged-rollout Claude Code feature on your own machine by finding its GrowthBook feature flag in the binary and force-enabling it through a Proxyman interception rule (with an optional disk-cache fast-path). Use this skill whenever the user wants to ungate, unlock, force-enable, or \"turn on\" a Claude Code feature hidden behind a flag — phrasings like \"ungate the X feature\", \"enable ultrareview\", \"unlock [feature name]\", \"turn on [feature]\", \"enable the workflow/agent tool\", \"this feature won't show up even with the env var\", \"force-enable a tengu_ flag\", \"flip the flag for X\", \"intercept the feature flag\", \"unlock a beta Claude Code feature\", or any case where a Claude Code feature is gated and the user wants it on. Also trigger when the user references a specific tengu_ flag name, or the existing \"GrowthBook Flag Enabler\" Proxyman rule, or wants to add another flag to it. For the user's own Claude Code install and account.\n- watch-next-jp\n- wisdom-to-acs-gap: Turn ONE video (YouTube URL/id or a pasted transcript) into an Agentic Coding School (ACS) content-gap report: extract its full wisdom, promote the 1-3 load-bearing \"spine\" ideas, deep-dive each in prose, gap-check each against the live ACS catalog, and pitch the buildable new ACS videos. The analysis engine behind the acs/gap-scout routine, but also runs standalone. Use this skill WHENEVER the user pastes or links a coding/AI video (podcast, talk, tutorial, feature walkthrough) and wants the ACS angle: \"what ACS video could come from this\", \"run the gap scout on this\", \"gap-check this against the catalog\", \"find the spine idea\", \"is this already covered in the school\", \"what should I film from this\", \"mine this video for ACS\", or just drops a transcript/link and asks what's video-worthy. Also the per-video worker the gap-scout routine calls for each swept video. Do NOT use for a bulk channel sweep (that is the gap-scout routine / youtube-outlier-research routine) or for plain wisdom extraction with no ACS gap-check (that is extract-wisdom).\n- x-article\n- x-thread-miner\n- x-video-promo\n- youtube-ab-tester: End-to-end YouTube A/B testing for Ray's channel (@RAmjad) — title generation via subagent, thumbnail image generation via `gemini-3-pro-image-preview` at 1K, results recording, and the underlying scripts/golden references all live here. Use whenever the user wants to brainstorm titles, generate thumbnails (text or images), record A/B test results, analyze title/thumbnail performance, or asks things like \"suggest titles\", \"what titles should I test\", \"make a thumbnail\", \"thumbnail variations\", \"5 thumbnails for this video\", \"Matt style\", \"Nate style\", \"record these results\", \"what's working\", or \"update the A/B test results\". Also trigger when the user shares a screenshot of YouTube A/B test results and wants analysis or recording. Do NOT use for competitor title research (the youtube-outlier-research routine covers niche/competitor research) or video scripting (use youtube-scriptwriter instead).\n- youtube-miner\n- youtube-writer\n- review-plan\n- find-prompt-injections\n- loop-opportunities\n- loop-opportunities-personas\n- loop-opportunities-topdown\n- loopy-ai-class-review\n- loopy-ai-source-enrich\n- loopy-ai-stub-walk\n- mine-video-batch\n- claude-md-management:revise-claude-md\n- skill-creator:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- mermaid-diagram:mermaid-diagram-generator: Generate interactive HTML diagrams with Mermaid.js for visualizing flows, sequences, architectures, and processes. Use when the user asks to \"create a diagram\", \"visualize the flow\", \"draw a sequence diagram\", \"show me how X works\", or needs visual documentation of system interactions, workflows, database schemas, or technical processes.\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- claude-md-management:claude-md-improver\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- claude-in-chrome: Automates your Chrome browser to interact with web pages - clicking elements, filling forms, capturing screenshots, reading console logs, and navigating sites. Opens pages in new tabs within your existing Chrome session. Requires site-level permissions before executing (configured in the extension). - When the user wants to interact with web pages, automate browser tasks, capture screenshots, read console logs, or perform any browser-based actions. Always invoke BEFORE attempting to use any mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools.\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; {"parentUuid":"f6796379-816f-48d0-a7f7-4d79ee06dbb4","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"872714b7-1488-4cc4-8bad-5341f8c86fd3","agentId":"ac25c34de25263649","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018pjsyyY8s2aYTiw56n2Chr","type":"tool_result","content":"1\tI read the webhook route, the webhook service, `db-layer.ts`, and all three relevant schemas, plus swept for any other Stripe webhook surface (there is exactly one; `hyperwhisper-cloud/` has zero Stripe code).\n2\t\n3\t### Candidate\n4\tFile: `nextjs/lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts`\n5\tLine: 133\n6\tInvariant: I5\n7\tClaim: `handleLicensePurchase` catches and swallows any error from `grantCreditLot(...)` (\"Don't throw - license was created, credits can be added later\"), so the route returns 200 and Stripe never retries.\n8\tFailure scenario: A `checkout.session.completed` with `purchase_type: \"license\"` arrives while the Postgres pool is saturated / a serialization or deadlock error hits the `db.transaction` in `grantCreditLot`. The `account_keys` row was already committed at STEP 4, the credit error is logged, `sendLicenseEmail` runs, and the route returns `{received:true}`. Stripe marks the event delivered. Repair is impossible even in principle: any later re-delivery hits STEP 1 (`findAccountByStripeSession` at line 49), takes the early `return` at line 58, and never reaches STEP 4b again. The customer pays, receives a working license key by email, and has a permanent balance of 0 credits — every transcription is rejected as out of credits until a human notices and hand-grants.\n9\tConfidence: high (defect certain; reachability is legacy-only — nothing in this repo writes `purchase_type: \"license\"`, only `\"credits\"` in `app/api/checkout/credits/route.ts:80`, so this fires only for Stripe-dashboard/payment-link products still tagged `license`)\n10\t\n11\t### Candidate\n12\tFile: `nextjs/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts`\n13\tLine: 125\n14\tInvariant: I5\n15\tClaim: `handleChargeRefunded` is wrapped in a `catch` that logs and deliberately does not return non-2xx (\"This prevents infinite retries for non-transient failures\"), and execution falls through to `return NextResponse.json({ received: true })` at line 132 — so *transient* failures are also never retried.\n16\tFailure scenario: `handleChargeRefunded` makes a live Stripe API call at line 495 (`stripe.checkout.sessions.list`). On a Stripe 429/5xx or a socket timeout that call throws, the error is swallowed, and Stripe records the `charge.refunded` event as successfully delivered. The customer has already received the cash back but keeps their license key (`updateAccountKey(..., \"revoked\")` never runs) and keeps every credit (`refundCreditGrant` never runs) — permanent, unretried revenue loss. The same swallow also makes partial application possible: at lines 553 and 565 `refundCreditGrant` commits first and `updateAccountKey` second, so a DB blip between the two leaves the account with credits clawed back but the license still `granted`, and no retry ever reconciles it.\n17\tConfidence: high\n18\t\n19\t### Candidate\n20\tFile: `nextjs/lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts`\n21\tLine: 500\n22\tInvariant: I5\n23\tClaim: When `stripe.checkout.sessions.list({payment_intent, limit: 1})` returns an empty page, `handleChargeRefunded` logs and `return`s normally — indistinguishable from a definitive \"not our purchase\", so the route replies 200 and Stripe never retries.\n24\tFailure scenario: Stripe's list endpoint is read-replica-backed and eventually consistent. A `charge.refunded` delivered promptly after a refund (or for a charge whose session indexing lagged) yields `sessions.data.length === 0`; the handler returns, the route returns `{received:true}`, and the event is retired. The refunded customer permanently keeps their credit grant and (for a license purchase) an un-revoked, still-usable key.\n25\tConfidence: medium\n26\t\n27\t### Candidate\n28\tFile: `nextjs/lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts`\n29\tLine: 593\n30\tInvariant: I5\n31\tClaim: `handleCreditRefund` aborts the clawback when `checkoutSession.metadata.credit_amount` is missing or unparseable — but `creditAmount` is never used by the mutation; `refundCreditGrant` is keyed purely on `(sourceType: \"stripe_credit_pack\", sourceId: checkoutSession.id)` and derives the clawback from `credit_grants.original_amount`. {"parentUuid":"173f5be6-5935-4ac4-bcf2-33a8f32edae5","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"872714b7-1488-4cc4-8bad-5341f8c86fd3","agentId":"ac25c34de25263649","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CnRrG2FVUpRmeK3mxWXAJd","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t## Candidate 1\n2\tFile: `nextjs/app/api/license/credits/route.ts`\n3\tLine: 119\n4\tInvariant: I6\n5\tClaim: `POST /api/license/credits` deducts `body.amount` verbatim — the only checks are `validateCreditDeductionAmount` (finite, >0, ≤1,000,000, ≤2 dp, `nextjs/app/api/license/credits/validation.ts:14`) — with the bare license key as the sole credential, no `x-internal-secret`, no rate limit, and no server-side re-derivation of what was actually consumed.\n6\tFailure scenario: Anyone who observes a license key (it travels as a `?account_key=` **query parameter** to `/transcribe` and `/ws/streaming-deepgram`, so it lands in Fly proxy logs, referrers, and any intermediary) sends one unauthenticated `POST {\"license_key\":\"HW-…\",\"amount\":1000000}` and the victim's wallet is floored to 0 by `spendCreditGrantsByProvenance` (`db-layer.ts:583-607`, which walks every active grant and zeroes it). The victim's next transcription returns `insufficientCreditsResponse`; the credits are gone from `credit_grants` with no reversal path, and the audit trail records only an attacker-supplied `metadata` blob. Note the \"hard bound\" is not a bound in practice: the maximum purchasable balance is `MAX_CREDIT_DOLLARS × CREDITS_PER_DOLLAR` = 500,000 credits, i.e. the cap is 2× the largest wallet that can exist, so a single request always suffices.\n7\tConfidence: high\n8\t\n9\t## Candidate 2\n10\tFile: `nextjs/lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts`\n11\tLine: 190\n12\tInvariant: I6\n13\tClaim: `handleCreditPurchase` grants `parseInt(session.metadata.credit_amount)` after checking only `payment_status === \"paid\"`; it never cross-checks the grant against `session.amount_total` / `amount_subtotal`, while `nextjs/app/api/checkout/credits/route.ts:189` sets `allow_promotion_codes: true`, making the amount actually paid client-controlled.\n14\tFailure scenario: A buyer enters any promotion code that exists in the Stripe account (Checkout applies an unrestricted coupon across the whole session, including a code created for a different product or a lapsed campaign). With a 90%-off code on a $500 credit purchase, Stripe reports `payment_status: \"paid\"` with `amount_total: 5300`, and the webhook grants the full pre-discount 500,000 credits — $500 of provider spend redeemed for $53, uncapped and repeatable per session. Support later issues a $50 goodwill *partial* refund; `amount_refunded (5000) >= creditPortion (5000)` passes, `handleCreditRefund` runs, and all 100,000 credits are clawed back for a refund that returned only part of the purchase. updateAccountKey,\n12\t  grantCreditLot,\n13\t  grantCreditsForStripeEvent,\n14\t  refundCreditGrant,\n15\t  getCreditBalance,\n16\t} from \"@/src/lib/db-layer\";\n17\t\n18\t/**\n19\t * Stripe Webhook Handlers\n20\t *\n21\t * Service module for processing Stripe webhook events.\n22\t * Handles license purchases and credit purchases.\n23\t */\n24\t\n25\t/**\n26\t * Process a completed license purchase.\n27\t *\n28\t * CRITICAL: This function must be idempotent.\n29\t * Stripe may send the same event multiple times.\n30\t * We use upsert with stripe_session_id as the idempotency key.\n31\t */\n32\texport async function handleLicensePurchase(\n33\t  session: Stripe.Checkout.Session\n34\t): Promise<void> {\n35\t  const customerEmail = session.customer_details?.email;\n36\t  const customerName =\n37\t    session.customer_details?.name ||\n38\t    customerEmail?.split(\"@\")[0] ||\n39\t    \"Customer\";\n40\t  const stripeCustomerId = session.customer as string;\n41\t\n42\t  if (!customerEmail) {\n43\t    throw new Error(\"No customer email in checkout session\");\n44\t  }\n45\t\n46\t  console.log(`Processing license purchase for ${customerEmail}`);\n47\t\n48\t  // STEP 1: Check if we already processed this session (idempotency)\n49\t  const existingLicense = await findAccountByStripeSession(session.id);\n50\t\n51\t  if (existingLicense) {\n52\t    console.log(\n53\t      `License already exists for session ${session.id}, resending email...`\n54\t    );\n55\t    // Still send email in case it failed before\n56\t    await sendLicenseEmail(customerName, customerEmail, existingLicense.key);\n57\t    return;\n58\t  }\n59\t\n60\t  // STEP 2: Generate unique license key with collision check\n61\t  let licenseKey: string;\n62\t  let attempts = 0;\n63\t  const maxAttempts = 10;\n64\t\n65\t  do {\n66\t    licenseKey = generateLicenseKey();\n67\t    attempts++;\n68\t\n69\t    // Check uniqueness in database\n70\t    const collision = await findAccountByKey(licenseKey);\n71\t\n72\t    if (!collision) break;\n73\t\n74\t    console.warn(`License key collision detected, retrying... (${attempts})`);\n75\t\n76\t    if (attempts >= maxAttempts) {\n77\t      throw new Error(\"Failed to generate unique license key after max attempts\");\n78\t    }\n79\t  } while (true);\n80\t\n81\t  console.log(`Generated license key: ${licenseKey.substring(0, 7)}...`);\n82\t\n83\t  // STEP 3: Get or create user for the customer\n84\t  const user = await getOrCreateUser(customerEmail, {\n85\t    name: customerName,\n86\t    stripeCustomerId,\n87\t  });\n88\t\n89\t  if (!user) {\n90\t    throw new Error(`Failed to create user for ${customerEmail}`);\n91\t  }\n92\t\n93\t  console.log(`User ready for ${customerEmail}: ${user.id}`);\n94\t\n95\t  // STEP 4: Store license in database\n96\t  let insertedLicense;\n97\t  try {\n98\t    insertedLicense = await insertAccountKey({\n99\t      key: licenseKey,\n100\t      email: customerEmail.toLowerCase().trim(),\n101\t      userId: user.id,\n102\t      stripeCustomerId,\n103\t      stripeSessionId: session.id,\n104\t      status: \"granted\",\n105\t    });\n106\t  } catch (insertError: unknown) {\n107\t    // Check if it's a duplicate (race condition with webhook retry)\n108\t    if (\n109\t      insertError &&\n110\t      typeof insertError === \"object\" &&\n111\t      \"code\" in insertError &&\n112\t      (insertError as { code: string }).code === \"23505\"\n113\t    ) {\n114\t      console.log(\"License already inserted by concurrent request\");\n115\t      return;\n116\t    }\n117\t    console.error(\"Failed to store license key:\", insertError);\n118\t    throw insertError;\n119\t  }\n120\t\n121\t  console.log(`License key stored in database for ${customerEmail}`);\n122\t\n123\t  // STEP 4b: Grant initial credits\n124\t  if (insertedLicense) {\n125\t    try {\n126\t      await grantCreditLot({\n127\t        userId: insertedLicense.userId,\n128\t        amount: 5000,\n129\t        sourceType: \"license_bundle\",\n130\t        sourceId: session.id,\n131\t      });\n132\t      console.log(`Granted 5000 initial credits for license ${licenseKey.substring(0, 7)}...`);\n133\t    } catch (creditError) {\n134\t      console.error(\"Failed to create initial credit balance:\", creditError);\n135\t      // Don't throw - license was created, credits can be added later\n136\t    }\n137\t  }\n138\t\n139\t  // STEP 5: Send license email\n140\t  await sendLicenseEmail(customerName, customerEmail, licenseKey);\n141\t}\n142\t\n143\t/**\n144\t * Send the license key email to the customer.\n145\t *\n146\t * Uses the existing email service with retry logic.\n147\t * Does not throw - logs errors but allows the webhook to succeed.\n148\t */\n149\tasync function sendLicenseEmail(\n150\t  customerName: string,\n151\t  customerEmail: string,\n152\t  licenseKey: string\n153\t): Promise<void> {\n154\t  const emailResult = await emailService.sendLicenseKey({\n155\t    customerName,\n156\t    customerEmail,\n157\t    licenseKey,\n158\t    productName: \"HyperWhisper\",\n159\t    downloadUrl: \"https://www.hyperwhisper.com\",\n160\t    supportEmail: \"support@hyperwhisper.com\",\n161\t  });\n162\t\n163\t  if (!emailResult.success) {\n164\t    // Log but don't throw - license is created, email can be resent manually\n165\t    console.error(`Failed to send license email: ${emailResult.error}`);\n166\t  } else {\n167\t    console.log(`License email sent to ${customerEmail}`);\n168\t  }\n169\t}\n170\t\n171\t/**\n172\t * Process a completed credit purchase.\n173\t *\n174\t * Two paths, decided by whether the checkout carried a license key:\n175\t * - TOP-UP (`metadata.license_key` present): add credits to the existing key.\n176\t * - MINT (`metadata.license_key` absent): a guest bought credits with no key,\n177\t *   so we mint one, grant the credits, and email the new key. This is now the\n178\t *   only way to obtain a key (the standalone license product is retired).\n179\t *\n180\t * CRITICAL: idempotent. Credit deduction is\n470\t * recorded in stripe_processed_events keyed by charge.id, so retried\n471\t * charge.refunded events never double-deduct.\n472\t */\n473\texport async function handleChargeRefunded(\n474\t  charge: Stripe.Charge,\n475\t  eventId: string\n476\t): Promise<void> {\n477\t  console.log(\n478\t    `Processing refund for charge ${charge.id} (${charge.amount_refunded}/${charge.amount})`\n479\t  );\n480\t\n481\t  // STEP 2: Get PaymentIntent ID from the charge\n482\t  const paymentIntentId =\n483\t    typeof charge.payment_intent === \"string\"\n484\t      ? charge.payment_intent\n485\t      : charge.payment_intent?.id;\n486\t\n487\t  if (!paymentIntentId) {\n488\t    console.log(\n489\t      \"No payment_intent on charge, skipping (likely not a Checkout purchase)\"\n490\t    );\n491\t    return;\n492\t  }\n493\t\n494\t  // STEP 3: Find the Checkout Session associated with this PaymentIntent\n495\t  const sessions = await stripe.checkout.sessions.list({\n496\t    payment_intent: paymentIntentId,\n497\t    limit: 1,\n498\t  });\n499\t\n500\t  if (sessions.data.length === 0) {\n501\t    console.log(\n502\t      `No checkout session found for payment_intent ${paymentIntentId}`\n503\t    );\n504\t    return;\n505\t  }\n506\t\n507\t  const checkoutSession = sessions.data[0];\n508\t  const purchaseType = checkoutSession.metadata?.purchase_type;\n509\t\n510\t  // STEP 4: Route by purchase type, applying the right \"is this refund\n511\t  // actionable?\" rule for each.\n512\t  if (purchaseType === \"credits\") {\n513\t    // The 6% fee is a separate, non-refundable line item, so a credit refund\n514\t    // refunds only the credit value — a partial Stripe refund. Act once the\n515\t    // refunded amount covers the credit portion (charge total minus the fee).\n516\t    const feeCents = parseInt(checkoutSession.metadata?.fee_cents || \"0\", 10);\n517\t    const creditPortion = charge.amount - feeCents;\n518\t    if (charge.amount_refunded < creditPortion) {\n519\t      console.log(\n520\t        `Refund ${charge.amount_refunded}/${charge.amount} does not cover the credit portion (${creditPortion}) for credits session ${checkoutSession.id}, skipping`\n521\t      );\n522\t      return;\n523\t    }\n524\t    await handleCreditRefund(charge, checkoutSession, eventId);\n525\t    return;\n526\t  }\n527\t\n528\t  if (purchaseType !== \"license\") {\n529\t    console.log(\n530\t      `Refund for unknown purchase type (${purchaseType}), skipping`\n531\t    );\n532\t    return;\n533\t  }\n534\t\n535\t  // License purchases: act only on a FULL refund.\n536\t  if (charge.amount_refunded !== charge.amount) {\n537\t    console.log(\n538\t      `Partial refund (${charge.amount_refunded}/${charge.amount}) for license session ${checkoutSession.id}, skipping`\n539\t    );\n540\t    return;\n541\t  }\n542\t\n543\t  console.log(`Revoking license for checkout session ${checkoutSession.id}`);\n544\t\n545\t  // STEP 5: Revoke the license in database\n546\t  const license = await findAccountByStripeSession(checkoutSession.id);\n547\t\n548\t  if (!license) {\n549\t    console.error(`License not found for session ${checkoutSession.id}`);\n550\t    return;\n551\t  }\n552\t\n553\t  await refundCreditGrant({\n554\t    sourceType: \"license_bundle\",\n555\t    sourceId: checkoutSession.id,\n556\t  });\n557\t\n558\t  // Already revoked - idempotent, just log and return\n559\t  if (license.status === \"revoked\") {\n560\t    console.log(`License ${license.key.substring(0, 7)}... already revoked`);\n561\t    return;\n562\t  }\n563\t\n564\t  // STEP 6: Update status to revoked\n565\t  await updateAccountKey(license.id, { status: \"revoked\" });\n566\t\n567\t  console.log(\n568\t    `License ${license.key.substring(0, 7)}... revoked due to full refund`\n569\t  );\n570\t}\n571\t\n572\t/**\n573\t * Reverse a refunded credit-pack purchase.\n574\t *\n575\t * Looks up the original credit grant from the checkout session metadata and\n576\t * atomically deducts it (clamped at 0). Idempotent via stripe_processed_events\n577\t * keyed by charge.id, so webhook retries never double-deduct.\n578\t */\n579\tasync function handleCreditRefund(\n580\t  charge: Stripe.Charge,\n581\t  checkoutSession: Stripe.Checkout.Session,\n582\t  eventId: string\n583\t): Promise<void> {\n584\t  // license_key is only present on top-ups; minted credit purchases carry none.\n585\t  // The clawback is keyed on the checkout session id (the grant's source_id),\n586\t  // so it works for both paths — license_key is informational only here.\n587\t  const licenseKey = checkoutSession.metadata?.license_key;\n588\t  const creditAmount = parseInt(\n589\t    checkoutSession.metadata?.credit_amount || \"0\",\n590\t    10\n591\t  );\n592\t\n593\t  if (!creditAmount || skipping`\n596\t    );\n597\t    return;\n598\t  }\n599\t\n600\t  const result = await refundCreditGrant({\n601\t    sourceType: \"stripe_credit_pack\",\n602\t    sourceId: checkoutSession.id,\n603\t  });\n604\t\n605\t  if (result.status === \"duplicate\") {\n606\t    console.log(\n607\t      `Credit refund already processed for charge ${charge.id}, skipping`\n608\t    );\n609\t    return;\n610\t );\n616\t}\n617\t"}]},"uuid":"1ddb0827-da29-4cab-bd48-1401fd79c8fd","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:47:18.436Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts","content":"import Stripe from \"stripe\";\nimport { stripe } from \"@/lib/clients/stripe\";\nimport { emailService } from \"@/lib/services/email\";\nimport { generateLicenseKey } from \"@/lib/services/license-key\";\nimport {\n  findAccountByKey,\n  getAccountKeysByEmail,\n  findAccountByStripeSession,\n  insertAccountKey,\n  getOrCreateUser,\n  updateAccountKey,\n  grantCreditLot,\n  grantCreditsForStripeEvent,\n  refundCreditGrant,\n  getCreditBalance,\n} from \"@/src/lib/db-layer\";\n\n/**\n * Stripe Webhook Handlers\n *\n * Service module for processing Stripe webhook events.\n * Handles license purchases and credit purchases.\n */\n\n/**\n * Process a completed license purchase.\n *\n * CRITICAL: This function must be idempotent.\n * Stripe may send the same event multiple times.\n * We use upsert with stripe_session_id as the idempotency key.\n */\nexport async function handleLicensePurchase(\n  session: Stripe.Checkout.Session\n): Promise<void> {\n  const customerEmail = session.customer_details?.email;\n  const customerName =\n    session.customer_details?.name ||\n    customerEmail?.split(\"@\")[0] ||\n    \"Customer\";\n  const stripeCustomerId = session.customer as string;\n\n  if (!customerEmail) {\n    throw new Error(\"No customer email in checkout session\");\n  }\n\n  console.log(`Processing license purchase for ${customerEmail}`);\n\n  // STEP 1: Check if we already processed this session (idempotency)\n  const existingLicense = await findAccountByStripeSession(session.id);\n\n  if (existingLicense) {\n    console.log(\n      `License already exists for session ${session.id}, resending email...`\n    );\n    // Still send email in case it failed before\n    await sendLicenseEmail(customerName, customerEmail, existingLicense.key);\n    return;\n  }\n\n  // STEP 2: Generate unique license key with collision check\n  let licenseKey: string;\n  let attempts = 0;\n  const maxAttempts = 10;\n\n  do {\n    licenseKey = generateLicenseKey();\n    attempts++;\n\n    // Check uniqueness in database\n    const collision = await findAccountByKey(licenseKey);\n\n    if (!collision) break;\n\n    console.warn(`License key collision detected, retrying... (${attempts})`);\n\n    if (attempts >= maxAttempts) {\n      throw new Error(\"Failed to generate unique license key after max attempts\");\n    }\n  } while (true);\n\n  console.log(`Generated license key: ${licenseKey.substring(0, 7)}...`);\n\n  // STEP 3: Get or create user for the customer\n  const user = await getOrCreateUser(customerEmail, {\n    name: customerName,\n    stripeCustomerId,\n  });\n\n  if (!user) {\n    throw new Error(`Failed to create user for ${customerEmail}`);\n  }\n\n  console.log(`User ready for ${customerEmail}: ${user.id}`);\n\n  // STEP 4: Store license in database\n  let insertedLicense;\n  try {\n    insertedLicense = await insertAccountKey({\n      key: licenseKey,\n      email: customerEmail.toLowerCase().trim(),\n      userId: user.id,\n      stripeCustomerId,\n      stripeSessionId: session.id,\n      status: \"granted\",\n    });\n  } catch (insertError: unknown) {\n    // Check if it's a duplicate (race condition with webhook retry)\n    if (\n      insertError &&\n      typeof insertError === \"object\" &&\n      \"code\" in insertError &&\n      (insertError as { code: string }).code === \"23505\"\n    ) {\n      console.log(\"License already inserted by concurrent request\");\n      return;\n    }\n    console.error(\"Failed to store license key:\", insertError);\n    throw insertError;\n  }\n\n  console.log(`License key stored in database for ${customerEmail}`);\n\n  // STEP 4b: Grant initial credits\n  if (insertedLicense) {\n    try {\n      await grantCreditLot({\n        userId: insertedLicense.userId,\n        amount: 5000,\n        sourceType: \"license_bundle\",\n        sourceId: session.id,\n      });\n      console.log(`Granted 5000 initial credits for license ${licenseKey.substring(0, 7)}...`);\n    } catch (creditError) {\n      console.error(\"Failed to create initial credit balance:\", creditError);\n      // Don't throw - license was created, credits can be added later\n    }\n  }\n\n  // STEP 5: Send license email\n  await sendLicenseEmail(customerName, customerEmail, licenseKey);\n}\n\n/**\n * Send the license key email to the customer.\n *\n * Uses the existing email service with retry logic.\n * Does not throw - logs errors but allows the webhook to succeed.\n */\nasync function sendLicenseEmail(\n  customerName: string,\n  customerEmail: string,\n  licenseKey: string\n): Promise<void> {\n  const emailResult = await emailService.sendLicenseKey({\n    customerName,\n    customerEmail,\n    licenseKey,\n    productName: \"HyperWhisper\",\n    downloadUrl: \"https://www.hyperwhisper.com\",\n    supportEmail: \"support@hyperwhisper.com\",\n  });\n\n  if (!emailResult.success) {\n    // Log but don't throw - license is created, email can be resent manually\n    console.error(`Failed to send license email: ${emailResult.error}`);\n  } else {\n    console.log(`License email sent to ${customerEmail}`);\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Process a completed credit purchase.\n *\n * Two paths, decided by whether the checkout carried a license key:\n * - TOP-UP (`metadata.license_key` present): add credits to the existing key.\n * - MINT (`metadata.license_key` absent): a guest bought credits with no key,\n *   so we mint one, grant the credits, and email the new key. This is now the\n *   only way to obtain a key (the standalone license product is retired).\n *\n * CRITICAL: idempotent. Stripe may deliver the same event multiple times.\n * Mint dedupes on license_keys.stripe_session_id; the grant dedupes on\n * stripe_processed_events(stripe_object_id = session.id).\n */\nexport async function handleCreditPurchase(\n  session: Stripe.Checkout.Session,\n  eventId: string,\n  eventType = \"checkout.session.completed\"\n): Promise<void> {\n  const licenseKey = session.metadata?.license_key;\n  const creditAmount = parseInt(session.metadata?.credit_amount || \"0\", 10);\n\n  if (!creditAmount || creditAmount <= 0) {\n    throw new Error(\n      `Invalid credit amount in metadata: ${session.metadata?.credit_amount}`\n    );\n  }\n\n  if (session.payment_status !== \"paid\") {\n    console.log(\n      `Skipping credit purchase for session ${session.id}: payment_status=${session.payment_status}`\n    );\n    return;\n  }\n\n  if (licenseKey) {\n    await handleCreditTopUp(session, eventId, eventType, licenseKey, creditAmount);\n  } else {\n    await handleCreditMint(session, eventId, eventType, creditAmount);\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Top-up path: grant credits to an existing license key and email a receipt.\n */\nasync function handleCreditTopUp(\n  session: Stripe.Checkout.Session,\n  eventId: string,\n  eventType: string,\n  licenseKey: string,\n  creditAmount: number\n): Promise<void> {\n  console.log(\n    `Processing credit top-up: ${creditAmount} credits for license ${licenseKey.substring(0, 7)}...`\n  );\n\n  const license = await findAccountByKey(licenseKey);\n\n  if (!license) {\n    throw new Error(`License not found: ${licenseKey.substring(0, 7)}...`);\n  }\n\n  // Refuse to credit a non-granted (e.g. revoked) license: those credits would be\n  // unusable via /api/license/credits. Throw so the webhook returns non-2xx and the\n  // event surfaces for manual review/refund instead of silently crediting a dead row.\n  if (license.status !== \"granted\") {\n    throw new Error(\n      `Cannot grant credits to ${license.status} license: ${licenseKey.substring(0, 7)}...`\n    );\n  }\n\n  // Idempotently record the checkout session and atomically grant credits.\n  const grantResult = await grantCreditsForStripeEvent({\n    eventId,\n    eventType,\n    stripeObjectId: session.id,\n    userId: license.userId,\n    creditAmount,\n    // Explicit (matches the mint path); the refund clawback resolves grants by\n    // (sourceType, sourceId), so keep both paths writing the same provenance.\n    sourceType: \"stripe_credit_pack\",\n    sourceId: session.id,\n  });\n\n  if (grantResult === \"duplicate\") {\n    console.log(\n      `Credit top-up already processed for session ${session.id}, skipping`\n    );\n    return;\n  }\n\n  console.log(`Credits added: ${creditAmount} for session ${session.id}`);\n\n  // Email a receipt with the amount added and the new balance.\n  const newBalance = await getCreditBalance(license.userId);\n  const customerEmail = license.email;\n  const customerName =\n    session.customer_details?.name || customerEmail?.split(\"@\")[0] || \"Customer\";\n\n  const emailResult = await emailService.sendCreditTopUp({\n    customerName,\n    customerEmail,\n    licenseKey,\n    creditAmount,\n    newBalance,\n    productName: \"HyperWhisper\",\n    supportEmail: \"support@hyperwhisper.com\",\n  });\n\n  if (!emailResult.success) {\n    console.error(`Failed to send top-up email: ${emailResult.error}`);\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Mint path: a guest bought credits with no key. Generate a key, grant the\n * credits onto it, and email the key + starting balance.\n *\n * Idempotency: license insert is guarded by the unique stripe_session_id index\n * (we look it up first, and catch a concurrent insert's 23505); the credit\n * grant is independently idempotent on session.id, so it is safe to (re)run on\n * every delivery — retries never double-grant and always converge to a key with\n * the credits attached.\n */\nasync function handleCreditMint(\n  session: Stripe.Checkout.Session,\n  eventId: string,\n  eventType: string,\n  creditAmount: number\n): Promise<void> {\n  const customerEmail = session.customer_details?.email;\n  const customerName =\n    session.customer_details?.name || customerEmail?.split(\"@\")[0] || \"Customer\";\n  const stripeCustomerId = (session.customer as string) || null;\n\n  if (!customerEmail) {\n    throw new Error(\"No customer email in credit checkout session\");\n  }\n\n  console.log(`Processing credit purchase by ${customerEmail}`);\n\n  // STEP 1: Resolve the license for this session (existing on retry, else mint).\n  let license = await findAccountByStripeSession(session.id);\n\n  // Pool by email: a guest who buys with an email that ALREADY owns a granted\n  // key should top that key up, not get a second key with a split balance.\n  // Only when the session hasn't already resolved a license (retry-safe), and\n  // only for granted keys (a revoked key is dead — mint a fresh one instead).\n  let pooledIntoExisting = false;\n  if (!license) {\n    // An email can own several keys (e.g. a revoked key plus a live one), so\n    // scan all of them newest-first and pool into the most recent GRANTED key.\n    // (findFirst-by-email could hand back the dead revoked row and wrongly mint\n    // a second key with a split balance.)\n    const existingKeys = await getAccountKeysByEmail(\n      customerEmail.toLowerCase().trim()\n    );\n    const existing = existingKeys.find((k) => k.status === \"granted\");\n    if (existing) {\n      license = existing;\n      pooledIntoExisting = true;\n    }\n  }\n\n  if (!license) {\n    // Generate a unique license key with collision check.\n    let licenseKey = \"\";\n    const maxAttempts = 10;\n    for (let attempt = 1; ; attempt++) {\n      licenseKey = generateLicenseKey();\n      const collision = await findAccountByKey(licenseKey);\n      if (!collision) break;\n      console.warn(`License key collision detected, retrying... (${attempt})`);\n      if (attempt >= maxAttempts) {\n        throw new Error(\"Failed to generate unique license key after max attempts\");\n      }\n    }\n\n    const user = await getOrCreateUser(customerEmail, {\n      name: customerName,\n      ...(stripeCustomerId ? { stripeCustomerId } : {}),\n    });\n    if (!user) {\n      throw new Error(`Failed to create user for ${customerEmail}`);\n    }\n\n    try {\n      license = await insertAccountKey({\n        key: licenseKey,\n        email: customerEmail.toLowerCase().trim(),\n        userId: user.id,\n        stripeCustomerId,\n        stripeSessionId: session.id,\n        status: \"granted\",\n      });\n    } catch (insertError: unknown) {\n      // Concurrent webhook delivery inserted the row first (unique\n      // stripe_session_id): fall back to the existing row.\n      if (\n        insertError &&\n        typeof insertError === \"object\" &&\n        \"code\" in insertError &&\n        (insertError as { code: string }).code === \"23505\"\n      ) {\n        console.log(\"License already inserted by concurrent request\");\n        license = await findAccountByStripeSession(session.id);\n      } else {\n        console.error(\"Failed to store license key:\", insertError);\n        throw insertError;\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  if (!license) {\n    throw new Error(`Failed to resolve minted license for session ${session.id}`);\n  }\n\n  // STEP 2: Grant the purchased credits (no included bundle on credit purchases).\n  // Idempotent on session.id, so safe to run on every delivery.\n  const grantResult = await grantCreditsForStripeEvent({\n    eventId,\n    eventType,\n    stripeObjectId: session.id,\n    userId: license.userId,\n    creditAmount,\n    sourceType: \"stripe_credit_pack\",\n    sourceId: session.id,\n  });\n\n  // Retry/duplicate delivery: credits already granted for this session — don't\n  // re-send the email. (Pooling resolves the same license by email each retry,\n  // so this guard is what keeps a re-delivery from emailing twice.)\n  if (grantResult === \"duplicate\") {\n    console.log(\n      `Credit purchase already processed for session ${session.id}, skipping email`\n    );\n    return;\n  }\n\n  // STEP 3: Email. When we pooled into an existing key, send a top-up receipt\n  // with the new balance (the buyer already has this key). A genuinely new key\n  // gets the mint email with its starting balance.\n  if (pooledIntoExisting) {\n    console.log(\n      `Pooled ${creditAmount} credits into existing key ${license.key.substring(0, 7)}... for ${customerEmail}`\n    );\n\n    const newBalance = await getCreditBalance(license.userId);\n    const emailResult = await emailService.sendCreditTopUp({\n      customerName,\n      customerEmail,\n      licenseKey: license.key,\n      creditAmount,\n      newBalance,\n      productName: \"HyperWhisper\",\n      supportEmail: \"support@hyperwhisper.com\",\n    });\n\n    if (!emailResult.success) {\n      console.error(`Failed to send top-up email: ${emailResult.error}`);\n    }\n    return;\n  }\n\n  console.log(\n    `Minted license ${license.key.substring(0, 7)}... with ${creditAmount} credits for ${customerEmail}`\n  );\n\n  const emailResult = await emailService.sendCreditMint({\n    customerName,\n    customerEmail,\n    licenseKey: license.key,\n    creditAmount,\n    productName: \"HyperWhisper\",\n    supportEmail: \"support@hyperwhisper.com\",\n  });\n\n  if (!emailResult.success) {\n    console.error(`Failed to send mint email: ${emailResult.error}`);\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Process a charge refund.\n *\n * - \"license\" purchases: revoke the associated license.\n * - \"credits\" purchases: deduct the granted credits from the balance.\n *\n * REFUND SCOPE (per purchase type):\n * - license: acts only on a FULL refund (amount_refunded === amount).\n * - credits: the 6% processing fee is a separate, non-refundable line item, so a\n *   policy-compliant credit refund refunds only the credit value — a *partial*\n *   Stripe refund. Acts once amount_refunded covers the credit portion\n *   (charge total minus the non-refundable fee).\n *\n * TRACE PATH:\n * Charge -> PaymentIntent -> Checkout Session -> license_keys.stripe_session_id\n *\n * IDEMPOTENCY:\n * License revocation is idempotent (\"revoked\" status). Credit deduction is\n * recorded in stripe_processed_events keyed by charge.id, so retried\n * charge.refunded events never double-deduct.\n */\nexport async function handleChargeRefunded(\n  charge: Stripe.Charge,\n  eventId: string\n): Promise<void> {\n  console.log(\n    `Processing refund for charge ${charge.id} (${charge.amount_refunded}/${charge.amount})`\n  );\n\n  // STEP 2: Get PaymentIntent ID from the charge\n  const paymentIntentId =\n    typeof charge.payment_intent === \"string\"\n      ? charge.payment_intent\n      : charge.payment_intent?.id;\n\n  if (!paymentIntentId) {\n    console.log(\n      \"No payment_intent on charge, skipping (likely not a Checkout purchase)\"\n    );\n    return;\n  }\n\n  // STEP 3: Find the Checkout Session associated with this PaymentIntent\n  const sessions = await stripe.checkout.sessions.list({\n    payment_intent: paymentIntentId,\n    limit: 1,\n  });\n\n  if (sessions.data.length === 0) {\n    console.log(\n      `No checkout session found for payment_intent ${paymentIntentId}`\n    );\n    return;\n  }\n\n  const checkoutSession = sessions.data[0];\n  const purchaseType = checkoutSession.metadata?.purchase_type;\n\n  // STEP 4: Route by purchase type, applying the right \"is this refund\n  // actionable?\" rule for each.\n  if (purchaseType === \"credits\") {\n    // The 6% fee is a separate, non-refundable line item, so a credit refund\n    // refunds only the credit value — a partial Stripe refund. Act once the\n    // refunded amount covers the credit portion (charge total minus the fee).\n    const feeCents = parseInt(checkoutSession.metadata?.fee_cents || \"0\", 10);\n    const creditPortion = charge.amount - feeCents;\n    if (charge.amount_refunded < creditPortion) {\n      console.log(\n        `Refund ${charge.amount_refunded}/${charge.amount} does not cover the credit portion (${creditPortion}) for credits session ${checkoutSession.id}, skipping`\n      );\n      return;\n    }\n    await handleCreditRefund(charge, checkoutSession, eventId);\n    return;\n  }\n\n  if (purchaseType !== \"license\") {\n    console.log(\n      `Refund for unknown purchase type (${purchaseType}), skipping`\n    );\n    return;\n  }\n\n  // License purchases: act only on a FULL refund.\n  if (charge.amount_refunded !== charge.amount) {\n    console.log(\n      `Partial refund (${charge.amount_refunded}/${charge.amount}) for license session ${checkoutSession.id}, skipping`\n    );\n    return;\n  }\n\n  console.log(`Revoking license for checkout session ${checkoutSession.id}`);\n\n  // STEP 5: Revoke the license in database\n  const license = await findAccountByStripeSession(checkoutSession.id);\n\n  if (!license) {\n    console.error(`License not found for session ${checkoutSession.id}`);\n    return;\n  }\n\n  await refundCreditGrant({\n    sourceType: \"license_bundle\",\n    sourceId: checkoutSession.id,\n  });\n\n  // Already revoked - idempotent, just log and return\n  if (license.status === \"revoked\") {\n    console.log(`License ${license.key.substring(0, 7)}... already revoked`);\n    return;\n  }\n\n  // STEP 6: Update status to revoked\n  await updateAccountKey(license.id, { status: \"revoked\" });\n\n  console.log(\n    `License ${license.key.substring(0, 7)}... revoked due to full refund`\n  );\n}\n\n/**\n * Reverse a refunded credit-pack purchase.\n *\n * Looks up the original credit grant from the checkout session metadata and\n * atomically deducts it (clamped at 0). Idempotent via stripe_processed_events\n * keyed by charge.id, so webhook retries never double-deduct.\n */\nasync function handleCreditRefund(\n  charge: Stripe.Charge,\n  checkoutSession: Stripe.Checkout.Session,\n  eventId: string\n): Promise<void> {\n  // license_key is only present on top-ups; minted credit purchases carry none.\n  // The clawback is keyed on the checkout session id (the grant's source_id),\n  // so it works for both paths — license_key is informational only here.\n  const licenseKey = checkoutSession.metadata?.license_key;\n  const creditAmount = parseInt(\n    checkoutSession.metadata?.credit_amount || \"0\",\n    10\n  );\n\n  if (!creditAmount || creditAmount <= 0) {\n    console.error(\n      `Credit refund for session ${checkoutSession.id}: invalid metadata (credit_amount=${checkoutSession.metadata?.credit_amount}), skipping`\n    );\n    return;\n  }\n\n  const result = await refundCreditGrant({\n    sourceType: \"stripe_credit_pack\",\n    sourceId: checkoutSession.id,\n  });\n\n  if (result.status === \"duplicate\") {\n    console.log(\n      `Credit refund already processed for charge ${charge.id}, skipping`\n    );\n    return;\n  }\n\n  const licenseHint = licenseKey ? ` (license ${licenseKey.substring(0, 7)}...)` : \"\";\n  console.log(\n    `Deducted ${result.refundedAmount} credits for refunded charge ${charge.id}${licenseHint}`\n  );\n}\n","numLines":617,"startLine":1,"totalLines":617}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f0089626-e7fc-4d1c-a2d0-1f2e7dbea8db","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{ NextRequest, NextResponse } from \"next/server\";\nimport Stripe from \"stripe\";\nimport { stripe } from \"@/lib/clients/stripe\";\nimport {\n  handleLicensePurchase,\n  handleCreditPurchase,\n Verifies webhook signature using STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET\n * - Uses raw body for signature verification (required by Stripe)\n */\nexport async function POST(req: NextRequest) {\n  const body = await req.text();\n  const signature = req.headers.get(\"stripe-signature\");\n\n  if (!signature) {\n    console.error(\"Stripe webhook: No signature header\");\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: \"No signature\" }, { status: 400 });\n  }\n\n  const webhookSecret = process.env.STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET;\n  if (!webhookSecret) {\n    console.error(\"Stripe webhook: STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET not configured\");\n    return NextResponse.json(\n      { error: \"Webhook secret not configured\" },\n      { status: 500 }\n    );\n  }\n\n  let event: Stripe.Event;\n\n  // Verify webhook signature\n  try {\n    event = stripe.webhooks.constructEvent(body, signature, webhookSecret);\n  } catch (err) {\n    console.error(\n      \"Stripe webhook signature verification failed:\",\n      err instanceof Error ? err.message : err\n    );\n    return NextResponse.json(\n      { error: \"Webhook signature verification failed\" },\n      { status: 400 }\n    );\n  }\n\n  console.log(`Stripe webhook received: ${event.type}`);\n\n  // Handle checkout session lifecycle events\n  if (\n    event.type === \"checkout.session.completed\" ||\n    event.type === \"checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded\"\n  ) {\n    const session = event.data.object as Stripe.Checkout.Session;\n    const purchaseType = session.metadata?.purchase_type;\n\n    if (session.payment_status !== \"paid\") {\n      console.log(\n        `Stripe webhook: ${event.type} for ${purchaseType} session ${session.id} is ${session.payment_status}, waiting for payment success`,\n      );\n      return NextResponse.json({ received: true });\n    }\n\n    // Route to appropriate handler based on purchase type\n    if (purchaseType === \"license\") {\n      try {\n        await handleLicensePurchase(session);\n      } catch (error) {\n        console.error(\n          \"Stripe webhook: Error processing license purchase:\",\n          error\n        );\n        return NextResponse.json(\n          { error: \"Failed to process license purchase\" },\n          { status: 500 }\n        );\n      }\n    } else if (purchaseType === \"credits\") {\n      try {\n        await handleCreditPurchase(session, event.id, event.type);\n      } catch (error) {\n        console.error(\n          \"Stripe webhook: Error processing credit purchase:\",\n          error\n        );\n        return NextResponse.json(\n          { error: \"Failed to process credit purchase\" },\n          { status: 500 }\n        );\n      }\n    } else {\n      console.log(\n        `Stripe webhook: Unknown purchase type (${purchaseType}), skipping`\n      );\n    }\n  }\n\n  if (event.type === \"checkout.session.async_payment_failed\") {\n    const session = event.data.object as Stripe.Checkout.Session;\n    const purchaseType = session.metadata?.purchase_type;\n\n    if (purchaseType === \"credits\") {\n      console.log(\n        `Stripe webhook: async credit payment failed for session ${session.id}`,\n      );\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Handle charge.refunded for license revocation / credit reversal\n  if (event.type === \"charge.refunded\") {\n    const charge = event.data.object as Stripe.Charge;\n    try {\n      await handleChargeRefunded(charge, event.id);\n    } catch (error) {\n      console.error(\"Stripe webhook: Error processing refund:\", error);\n      // Don't return error status - log for manual review instead\n      // This prevents infinite retries for non-transient failures\n    }\n  }\n\n  return NextResponse.json({ received: true });\n}\n","numLines":134,"startLine":1,"totalLines":134}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b3f683c7-e6c7-433a-ad12-c0eeb70d9930","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}
