balance: number }> {\n379\t  const expiresAt = new Date(Date.now() + CREDIT_GRANT_TTL_MS);\n380\t  const [grantRow] = await tx\n381\t    .insert(creditGrants)\n382\t    .values({\n383\t      userId: data.userId,\n384\t      sourceType: data.sourceType,\n385\t      sourceId: data.sourceId,\n386\t      originalAmount: data.amount.toString(),\n387\t      remainingAmount: data.amount.toString(),\n388\t      refundedAmount: \"0\",\n389\t      status: \"active\",\n390\t      expiresAt,\n391\t    })\n392\t    .onConflictDoNothing({\n393\t      target: [creditGrants.sourceType, creditGrants.sourceId],\n394\t    })\n395\t    .returning({ id: creditGrants.id });\n396\t\n397\t  if (!grantRow) {\n398\t    // Read inside the transaction so the balance reflects the locked, in-tx\n399\t    // view rather than a stale snapshot from a second pooled connection.\n400\t    const balance = await getActiveGrantsTotal(tx, data.userId);\n401\t    return { status: \"duplicate\", balance };\n402\t  }\n403\t\n404\t  const balance = await incrementCreditBalance(tx, data.userId, data.amount);\n405\t  return { status: \"processed\", balance };\n406\t}\n407\t\n408\texport async function grantCreditLot(\n409\t  data: CreditGrantInsert\n410\t): Promise<{ status: \"processed\" | \"duplicate\"; balance: number }> {\n411\t  return db.transaction((tx) => grantCreditLotInTransaction(tx, data));\n412\t}\n413\t\n414\texport async function grantCreditsForStripeEvent(\n415\t  data: StripeCreditGrantInsert\n416\t): Promise<\"processed\" | \"duplicate\"> {\n417\t  const insertedEvent = await db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n418\t    const [eventRow] = await tx\n419\t      .insert(stripeProcessedEvents)\n420\t      .values({\n421\t        eventId: data.eventId,\n422\t        eventType: data.eventType,\n423\t        stripeObjectId: data.stripeObjectId,\n424\t      })\n425\t      .onConflictDoNothing({ target: stripeProcessedEvents.stripeObjectId })\n426\t      .returning({ eventId: stripeProcessedEvents.eventId });\n427\t\n428\t    if (!eventRow) {\n429\t      return null;\n430\t    }\n431\t\n432\t    await grantCreditLotInTransaction(tx, {\n433\t      userId: data.userId,\n434\t      amount: data.creditAmount,\n435\t      sourceType: data.sourceType ?? \"stripe_credit_pack\",\n436\t      sourceId: data.sourceId ?? data.stripeObjectId,\n437\t    });\n438\t\n439\t    return eventRow;\n440\t  });\n441\t\n442\t  return insertedEvent ? \"processed\" : \"duplicate\";\n443\t}\n444\t\n445\texport async function refundCreditGrant(\n446\t  data: CreditGrantRefund\n447\t): Promise<{ status: \"processed\" | \"duplicate\"; refundedAmount: number }> {\n448\t  return db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n449\t    // Resolve the refunded purchase by provenance. We deliberately do NOT filter\n450\t    // on remaining_amount > 0: a fully-consumed pack still has to be clawed back,\n451\t    // otherwise spending the pack first (so a refund can't hand back unused paid\n452\t    // credits — see spendCreditGrantsByProvenance / #872) would let a refund of a\n453\t    // fully-spent pack reclaim nothing, leaving the user with the paid usage for\n454\t    // free PLUS the cash refund (the inverse money-loss bug).\n455\t    const target = await tx.execute<{\n456\t      id: string;\n457\t      user_id: string;\n458\t      original_amount: string;\n459\t      refunded_amount: string;\n460\t    }>(sql`\n461\t      SELECT id, user_id, original_amount, refunded_amount\n462\t      FROM credit_grants\n463\t      WHERE source_type = ${data.sourceType}\n464\t        AND source_id = ${data.sourceId}\n465\t      FOR UPDATE\n466\t    `);\n467\t\n468\t    const grant = target.rows[0];\n469\t    if (!grant) {\n470\t      return { status: \"duplicate\", refundedAmount: 0 };\n471\t    }\n472\t\n473\t    // The amount this refund reclaims is the grant's full purchased value that\n474\t    // has not already been refunded. A refunded grant (refunded_amount already\n475\t    // covers original_amount) is a no-op / duplicate webhook delivery.\n476\t    const originalAmount = Number(grant.original_amount);\n477\t    const alreadyRefunded = Number(grant.refunded_amount);\n478\t    const clawback = originalAmount - alreadyRefunded;\n479\t    if (clawback <= 0) {\n480\t      return { status: \"duplicate\", refundedAmount: 0 };\n481\t    }\n482\t\n483\t    // A full refund must remove the FULL purchased value from the license's\n484\t    // spendable balance, regardless of which grant happened to be spent for the\n485\t    // matching usage. Clamped at the license's current active total so a user who\n486\t    // already burned the credits doesn't go negative, but no value escapes:\n487\t    //   spend order + refund clawback are reconciled against the SAME running\n488\t    //   total, so neither bundle-first nor pack-first ordering can leak money.\n489\t    let toClawback = Math.min(\n490\t      clawback,\n491\t      await getActiveGrantsTotal(tx, grant.user_id)\n492\t    );\n493\t\n494\t    // Draw the clawback down from the license's active, unexpired grants,\n495\t    // starting with the refunded grant itself, then the same oldest-first order\n496\t    // used when spending (soonest-to-expire first). The clawback total is\n497\t    // clamped at the license's current active balance, and both spend and\n498\t    // clawback reconcile against that same running total, so no ordering can\n499\t    // leak money. Expired grants are skipped: they no longer back any spendable\n500\t    // balance, so clawing them back would remove value that was never available.\n501\t    const drawdown = await tx.execute<{ id: string; remaining_amount: string }>(sql`\n502\t      SELECT id, remaining_amount\n503\t      FROM credit_grants\n504\t      WHERE user_id = ${grant.user_id}\n505\t        AND remaining_amount > 0\n506\t        AND status = 'active'\n507\t        AND ${ACTIVE_GRANT_EXPIRY}\n508\t      ORDER BY\n509\t        CASE WHEN id = ${grant.id} THEN 0 ELSE 1 END,\n510\t        expires_at ASC,\n511\t        created_at ASC,\n512\t        id\n513\t      FOR UPDATE\n514\t    `);\n515\t\n516\t    for (const row of drawdown.rows) {\n517\t      if (toClawback <= 0) break;\n518\t      const rowRemaining = Number(row.remaining_amount);\n519\t      const deduction = Math.min(rowRemaining, toClawback);\n520\t      if (deduction <= 0) continue;\n521\t      const newRemaining = rowRemaining - deduction;\n522\t      await tx\n523\t        .update(creditGrants)\n524\t        .set({\n525\t          remainingAmount: newRemaining.toString(),\n526\t          status: newRemaining === 0 ? \"spent\" : \"active\",\n527\t          updatedAt: new Date(),\n528\t        })\n529\t        .where(eq(creditGrants.id, row.id));\n530\t      toClawback -= deduction;\n531\t    }\n532\t\n533\t    // Mark the refunded grant as refunded and record the full reclaimed value on\n534\t    // it (even when the credits were physically drawn from other grants), so the\n535\t    // grant's lifetime is correct and a duplicate refund webhook is a no-op.\n536\t    await tx\n537\t      .update(creditGrants)\n538\t      .set({\n539\t        remainingAmount: \"0\",\n540\t        refundedAmount: (alreadyRefunded + clawback).toString(),\n541\t        status: \"refunded\",\n542\t        updatedAt: new Date(),\n543\t      })\n544\t      .where(eq(creditGrants.id, grant.id));\n545\t\n546\t    // Reconcile the cache to the authoritative grant total, healing any drift.\n547\t    await reconcileCreditBalance(tx, grant.user_id);\n548\t    return {\n549\t      status: \"processed\",\n550\t      refundedAmount: clawback,\n551\t    };\n552\t  });\n553\t}\n554\t\n555\texport async function spendCreditGrantsByProvenance(\n556\t  userId: string,\n557\t  amount: number\n558\t): Promise<{ balance: number; deductedAmount: number }> {\n559\t  return db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n560\t    const result = await tx.execute<{ id: string; remaining_amount: string }>(sql`\n561\t      SELECT id, remaining_amount\n562\t      FROM credit_grants\n563\t      WHERE user_id = ${userId}\n564\t        AND remaining_amount > 0\n565\t        AND status = 'active'\n566\t        AND ${ACTIVE_GRANT_EXPIRY}\n567\t      -- Spend OLDEST-FIRST: soonest-to-expire grants are consumed before grants\n568\t      -- that still have time on them (and never-expiring grants last), so a user\n569\t      -- naturally burns down credits before they lapse. This replaces the older\n570\t      -- provenance (paid-pack-first) order; refund safety is now preserved a\n571\t      -- different way — refundCreditGrant clamps every clawback at the license's\n572\t      -- current active balance and reconciles against the same running total\n573\t      -- (#872 / spec §6), so neither spend nor clawback ordering can leak money\n574\t      -- regardless of which grant a given unit of usage drew from. Expired\n575\t      -- grants are excluded above and never spent.\n576\t      ORDER BY\n577\t        expires_at ASC,\n578\t        created_at ASC,\n579\t        id\n580\t      FOR UPDATE\n581\t    `);\n582\t\n583\t    let remainingToDeduct = amount;\n584\t    let deductedAmount = 0;\n585\t\n586\t    for (let index = 0; index < result.rows.length; index++) {\n587\t      if (remainingToDeduct <= 0) break;\n588\t\n589\t      const grant = result.rows[index];\n590\t      const grantRemaining = Number(grant.remaining_amount);\n591\t      const deduction = Math.min(grantRemaining, remainingToDeduct);\n592\t\n593\t      if (deduction <= 0) continue;\n594\t\n595\t      const newRemaining = grantRemaining - deduction;\n596\t      await tx\n597\t        .update(creditGrants)\n598\t        .set({\n599\t          remainingAmount: newRemaining.toString(),\n600\t          status: newRemaining === 0 ? \"spent\" : \"active\",\n601\t          updatedAt: new Date(),\n602\t        })\n603\t        .where(eq(creditGrants.id, grant.id));\n604\t\n605\t      remainingToDeduct -= deduction;\n606\t      deductedAmount += deduction;\n607\t    }\n608\t\n609\t    // Reconcile the cached balance to the authoritative grant total in the same\n610\t    // transaction (and same locked view). This both (a) reads via `tx` instead\n611\t    // of a second pooled connection, and (b) self-heals any drift between the\n612\t    // credit_balances cache and the grant rows, so the cache can never report\n613\t    // spendable credits the grants can't actually back, or vice versa.\n614\t    const balance = await reconcileCreditBalance(tx, userId);\n615\t\n616\t    return { balance, deductedAmount };\n617\t  });\n618\t}\n619\t"}]},"uuid":"49a6daa9-c871-4df7-9e7d-d51dd8046396","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:47:35.467Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/src/lib/db-layer.ts","content":"        balance: sql`${creditBalances.balance} + ${amount}`,\n        updatedAt: new Date(),\n      },\n    })\n    .returning({ balance: creditBalances.balance });\n\n  return Number(row.balance);\n}\n\n// Every credit grant expires one year after it is created (OpenRouter model):\n// the ToS reserves the right to expire unused credits 365 days after purchase.\n// Stamped here so it covers every insert path (paid packs, minted keys, admin\n// grants); enforcement is the lazy `expires_at > now()` filter on reads/spends.\nconst CREDIT_GRANT_TTL_MS = 365 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;\n\nasync function grantCreditLotInTransaction(\n  tx: Parameters<Parameters<typeof db.transaction>[0]>[0],\n  data: CreditGrantInsert\n): Promise<{ status: \"processed\" | \"duplicate\"; balance: number }> {\n  const expiresAt = new Date(Date.now() + CREDIT_GRANT_TTL_MS);\n  const [grantRow] = await tx\n    .insert(creditGrants)\n    .values({\n      userId: data.userId,\n      sourceType: data.sourceType,\n      sourceId: data.sourceId,\n      originalAmount: data.amount.toString(),\n      remainingAmount: data.amount.toString(),\n      refundedAmount: \"0\",\n      status: \"active\",\n      expiresAt,\n    })\n    .onConflictDoNothing({\n      target: [creditGrants.sourceType, creditGrants.sourceId],\n    })\n    .returning({ id: creditGrants.id });\n\n  if (!grantRow) {\n    // Read inside the transaction so the balance reflects the locked, in-tx\n    // view rather than a stale snapshot from a second pooled connection.\n    const balance = await getActiveGrantsTotal(tx, data.userId);\n    return { status: \"duplicate\", balance };\n  }\n\n  const balance = await incrementCreditBalance(tx, data.userId, data.amount);\n  return { status: \"processed\", balance };\n}\n\nexport async function grantCreditLot(\n  data: CreditGrantInsert\n): Promise<{ status: \"processed\" | \"duplicate\"; balance: number }> {\n  return db.transaction((tx) => grantCreditLotInTransaction(tx, data));\n}\n\nexport async function grantCreditsForStripeEvent(\n  data: StripeCreditGrantInsert\n): Promise<\"processed\" | \"duplicate\"> {\n  const insertedEvent = await db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n    const [eventRow] = await tx\n      .insert(stripeProcessedEvents)\n      .values({\n        eventId: data.eventId,\n        eventType: data.eventType,\n        stripeObjectId: data.stripeObjectId,\n      })\n      .onConflictDoNothing({ target: stripeProcessedEvents.stripeObjectId })\n      .returning({ eventId: stripeProcessedEvents.eventId });\n\n    if (!eventRow) {\n      return null;\n    }\n\n    await grantCreditLotInTransaction(tx, {\n      userId: data.userId,\n      amount: data.creditAmount,\n      sourceType: data.sourceType ?? \"stripe_credit_pack\",\n      sourceId: data.sourceId ?? data.stripeObjectId,\n    });\n\n    return eventRow;\n  });\n\n  return insertedEvent ? \"processed\" : \"duplicate\";\n}\n\nexport async function refundCreditGrant(\n  data: CreditGrantRefund\n): Promise<{ status: \"processed\" | \"duplicate\"; refundedAmount: number }> {\n  return db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n    // Resolve the refunded purchase by provenance. We deliberately do NOT filter\n    // on remaining_amount > 0: a fully-consumed pack still has to be clawed back,\n    // otherwise spending the pack first (so a refund can't hand back unused paid\n    // credits — see spendCreditGrantsByProvenance / #872) would let a refund of a\n    // fully-spent pack reclaim nothing, leaving the user with the paid usage for\n    // free PLUS the cash refund (the inverse money-loss bug).\n    const target = await tx.execute<{\n      id: string;\n      user_id: string;\n      original_amount: string;\n      refunded_amount: string;\n    }>(sql`\n      SELECT id, user_id, original_amount, refunded_amount\n      FROM credit_grants\n      WHERE source_type = ${data.sourceType}\n        AND source_id = ${data.sourceId}\n      FOR UPDATE\n    `);\n\n    const grant = target.rows[0];\n    if (!grant) {\n      return { status: \"duplicate\", refundedAmount: 0 };\n    }\n\n    // The amount this refund reclaims is the grant's full purchased value that\n    // has not already been refunded. A refunded grant (refunded_amount already\n    // covers original_amount) is a no-op / duplicate webhook delivery.\n    const originalAmount = Number(grant.original_amount);\n    const alreadyRefunded = Number(grant.refunded_amount);\n    const clawback = originalAmount - alreadyRefunded;\n    if (clawback <= 0) {\n      return { status: \"duplicate\", refundedAmount: 0 };\n    }\n\n    // A full refund must remove the FULL purchased value from the license's\n    // spendable balance, regardless of which grant happened to be spent for the\n    // matching usage. Clamped at the license's current active total so a user who\n    // already burned the credits doesn't go negative, but no value escapes:\n    //   spend order + refund clawback are reconciled against the SAME running\n    //   total, so neither bundle-first nor pack-first ordering can leak money.\n    let toClawback = Math.min(\n      clawback,\n      await getActiveGrantsTotal(tx, grant.user_id)\n    );\n\n    // Draw the clawback down from the license's active, unexpired grants,\n    // starting with the refunded grant itself, then the same oldest-first order\n    // used when spending (soonest-to-expire first). The clawback total is\n    // clamped at the license's current active balance, and both spend and\n    // clawback reconcile against that same running total, so no ordering can\n    // leak money. Expired grants are skipped: they no longer back any spendable\n    // balance, so clawing them back would remove value that was never available.\n    const drawdown = await tx.execute<{ id: string; remaining_amount: string }>(sql`\n      SELECT id, remaining_amount\n      FROM credit_grants\n      WHERE user_id = ${grant.user_id}\n        AND remaining_amount > 0\n        AND status = 'active'\n        AND ${ACTIVE_GRANT_EXPIRY}\n      ORDER BY\n        CASE WHEN id = ${grant.id} THEN 0 ELSE 1 END,\n        expires_at ASC,\n        created_at ASC,\n        id\n      FOR UPDATE\n    `);\n\n    for (const row of drawdown.rows) {\n      if (toClawback <= 0) break;\n      const rowRemaining = Number(row.remaining_amount);\n      const deduction = Math.min(rowRemaining, toClawback);\n      if (deduction <= 0) continue;\n      const newRemaining = rowRemaining - deduction;\n      await tx\n        .update(creditGrants)\n        .set({\n          remainingAmount: newRemaining.toString(),\n          status: newRemaining === 0 ? \"spent\" : \"active\",\n          updatedAt: new Date(),\n        })\n        .where(eq(creditGrants.id, row.id));\n      toClawback -= deduction;\n    }\n\n    // Mark the refunded grant as refunded and record the full reclaimed value on\n    // it (even when the credits were physically drawn from other grants), so the\n    // grant's lifetime is correct and a duplicate refund webhook is a no-op.\n    await tx\n      .update(creditGrants)\n      .set({\n        remainingAmount: \"0\",\n        refundedAmount: (alreadyRefunded + clawback).toString(),\n        status: \"refunded\",\n        updatedAt: new Date(),\n      })\n      .where(eq(creditGrants.id, grant.id));\n\n    // Reconcile the cache to the authoritative grant total, healing any drift.\n    await reconcileCreditBalance(tx, grant.user_id);\n    return {\n      status: \"processed\",\n      refundedAmount: clawback,\n    };\n  });\n}\n\nexport async function spendCreditGrantsByProvenance(\n  userId: string,\n  amount: number\n): Promise<{ balance: number; deductedAmount: number }> {\n  return db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n    const result = await tx.execute<{ id: string; remaining_amount: string }>(sql`\n      SELECT id, remaining_amount\n      FROM credit_grants\n      WHERE user_id = ${userId}\n        AND remaining_amount > 0\n        AND status = 'active'\n        AND ${ACTIVE_GRANT_EXPIRY}\n      -- Spend OLDEST-FIRST: soonest-to-expire grants are consumed before grants\n      -- that still have time on them (and never-expiring grants last), so a user\n      -- naturally burns down credits before they lapse. This replaces the older\n      -- provenance (paid-pack-first) order; refund safety is now preserved a\n      -- different way — refundCreditGrant clamps every clawback at the license's\n      -- current active balance and reconciles against the same running total\n      -- (#872 / spec §6), so neither spend nor clawback ordering can leak money\n      -- regardless of which grant a given unit of usage drew from. Expired\n      -- grants are excluded above and never spent.\n      ORDER BY\n        expires_at ASC,\n        created_at ASC,\n        id\n      FOR UPDATE\n    `);\n\n    let remainingToDeduct = amount;\n    let deductedAmount = 0;\n\n    for (let index = 0; index < result.rows.length; index++) {\n      if (remainingToDeduct <= 0) break;\n\n      const grant = result.rows[index];\n      const grantRemaining = Number(grant.remaining_amount);\n      const deduction = Math.min(grantRemaining, remainingToDeduct);\n\n      if (deduction <= 0) continue;\n\n      const newRemaining = grantRemaining - deduction;\n      await tx\n        .update(creditGrants)\n        .set({\n          remainingAmount: newRemaining.toString(),\n          status: newRemaining === 0 ? \"spent\" : \"active\",\n          updatedAt: new Date(),\n        })\n        .where(eq(creditGrants.id, grant.id));\n\n      remainingToDeduct -= deduction;\n      deductedAmount += deduction;\n    }\n\n    // Reconcile the cached balance to the authoritative grant total in the same\n    // transaction (and same locked view). This both (a) reads via `tx` instead\n    // of a second pooled connection, and (b) self-heals any drift between the\n    // credit_balances cache and the grant rows, so the cache can never report\n    // spendable credits the grants can't actually back, or vice versa.\n    const balance = await reconcileCreditBalance(tx, userId);\n\n    return { balance, deductedAmount };\n  });\n}\n","numLines":260,"startLine":360,"totalLines":1024}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"1896cab2-b27b-4182-933e-1c05005a078d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

}\n29\t  return null;\n30\t}\n31\t\n32\texport interface CreditPurchaseBreakdown {\n33\t  /** Credits granted: amount * CREDITS_PER_DOLLAR. */\n34\t  creditAmount: number;\n35\t  /** Credit line-item * Pure validation + pricing helpers for credit purchases.\n *\n * Kept free of Next/Stripe imports so they can be unit-tested directly (see\n * tests/credit-purchase-validation.test.ts) and reused by the checkout route.\n */\n\nexport const MIN_CREDIT_DOLLARS = 5;\nexport const MAX_CREDIT_DOLLARS = 500;\n/** 1,000 credits per US$1. */\nexport const CREDITS_PER_DOLLAR = 1000;\n/** Non-refundable processing fee added on top of the credit value. */\nexport const CREDIT_FEE_RATE = 0.06;\n\n/**\n * Validate the requested purchase amount (whole US dollars, MIN..MAX inclusive).\n * Returns an error string, or null when valid.\n */\nexport function validateCreditPurchaseAmount(amount: unknown): string | null {\n  if (typeof amount !== \"number\" || !Number.isFinite(amount)) {\n    return \"amount must be a finite number\";\n  }\n  if (!Number.isInteger(amount)) {\n    return \"amount must be a whole number of dollars\";\n  }\n  if (amount < MIN_CREDIT_DOLLARS || amount > MAX_CREDIT_DOLLARS) {\n    return `amount must be between ${MIN_CREDIT_DOLLARS} and ${MAX_CREDIT_DOLLARS} dollars`;\n  }\n  return null;\n}\n\nexport interface CreditPurchaseBreakdown {\n  /** Credits granted: amount * CREDITS_PER_DOLLAR. */\n  creditAmount: number;\n  /** Credit line-item amount in cents: amount * 100. */\n  creditCents: number;\n  /** Processing fee in cents: round(creditCents * CREDIT_FEE_RATE). */\n  feeCents: number;\n}\n\n/**\n * Compute the Stripe line-item breakdown for a validated dollar amount.\n * Caller must pass an amount that already passed validateCreditPurchaseAmount.\n */\nexport function computeCreditPurchase(amount: number): CreditPurchaseBreakdown {\n  const creditCents = amount * 100;\n  return {\n    creditAmount: amount * CREDITS_PER_DOLLAR,\n    creditCents,\n    feeCents: Math.round(creditCents * CREDIT_FEE_RATE),\n  };\n}\n","numLines":53,"startLine":1,"totalLines":53}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d30375c3-a8fd-4f44-b5ca-214b13f6c5a0","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"ae487832-302e-480d-bdda-be2ee7ae3d5a","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"ac25c34de25263649","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdWJPEmNCs7btMf5t5KMQ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"## Verdicts\n\n---\n\n**`[nextjs/lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts:133]` REFUTED — dead code (legacy branch, no live entry path)**\n\nThe finder's own caveat is correct and I confirmed it is decisive. `handleLicensePurchase` has exactly one caller:\n\n```ts\n// app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:76-78\nif (purchaseType === \"license\") {\n  await handleLicensePurchase(session);\n```\n\n`grep -rn \"purchase_type\"` over the whole tree returns **one writer**, `app/api/checkout/credits/route.ts:80`, which writes `purchase_type: \"credits\"`. Nothing in the current tree can produce a session with `purchase_type: \"license\"`.\n\nGit history shows why: `nextjs/app/api/checkout/license-key/route.ts` was the writer (`metadata: { purchase_type: \"license\", ... }`, line 71 at `e3708d4`) and was **deleted in commit `9b53d84`** (\"Cloud credits: wallet backend + pricing & guest buy-credits page\"). The service docblock states the intent explicitly (`stripe-webhook.ts:176-178`):\n\n> `This is now the only way to obtain a key (the standalone license product is retired).`\n\nThe swallow itself is real and its unrecoverability analysis is correct (STEP 1's `findAccountByStripeSession` early-`return` at :51-58 does permanently prevent a retry from re-reaching STEP 4b) — but it is a defect in a branch nothing can enter. It would fire only if someone created a Stripe-dashboard product/payment-link tagged `purchase_type: \"license\"`, which no repo artefact does. Correct disposition: **dead legacy code, delete rather than fix.**\n\n---\n\n**`[nextjs/lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts:190]` PLAUSIBLE — but *not* an I6 violation; the mechanism is \"grant unreconciled against amount paid\"**\n\nTwo separate questions, and the finder conflated them.\n\n*Is `credit_amount` client-controlled?* **No.** It is server-derived and hard-bounded. `app/api/checkout/credits/route.ts:46-53` runs `validateCreditPurchaseAmount` (whole dollars, `MIN_CREDIT_DOLLARS=5` .. `MAX_CREDIT_DOLLARS=500`, `checkout/credits/validation.ts:19-30`) then `computeCreditPurchase(amount)`, and `credit_amount` is written as `creditAmount.toString()` (`route.ts:81`). Both Stripe line items take `unit_amount` from the same computation. A client cannot forge session metadata without the Stripe secret key. **I6 as written is satisfied at this line** — the pattern the invariant targets (client picks the number, server trusts it) does not exist here.\n\n*Is the grant reconciled against money received?* **No, and that gap is real.** The only pre-grant check is `session.payment_status !== \"paid\"` (:198); `session.amount_total` / `amount_subtotal` are never read anywhere in the file. Combined with `allow_promotion_codes: true` (`checkout/credits/route.ts:189`), a discount decouples cash received from credits granted.\n\nQuantifying the lever, which the finder did not:\n- A **100%-off** coupon does **not** work: Stripe sets `payment_status: \"no_payment_required\"` (not `\"paid\"`) on a zero-total session, so :198 returns early. That is an accidental but effective barrier at exactly 100%.\n- Anything **below** 100% does work. A 90%-off code on the $500 cap yields `amount_total ≈ 5300`, `payment_status: \"paid\"`, and a full 500,000-credit grant — repeatable per session, and the checkout route is unauthenticated and unrate-limited (I7).\n- Because both line items use inline `price_data` with ad-hoc products (no `product` id), a coupon *restricted* by product cannot match them. Only unrestricted coupons apply — which inverts the finder's \"a code created for a different product\" reasoning, but leaves the exposure.\n\nThe finder's \"a lapsed campaign\" leg is wrong: an expired or `active:false` promotion code is rejected by Checkout.\n\n**Exact unproven link:** whether any *active, unrestricted, non-100%* promotion code exists in the live Stripe account. The repo contains **zero** coupon/promotion-code creation code (`grep -rni \"coupon\\|promotion_code\\|allow_promotion\"` hits only line 189 itself and marketing copy). Confirm by listing `active: true` promotion codes in the Stripe dashboard. If none exist, this is latent only.\n\nNote the intent signal cuts against escalation: `allow_promotion_codes: true` is deliberate (comment `// Allow coupon/promotion codes`), and giving a discount is the merchant's own pricing act — the \"loss\" is the discount the merchant chose to offer. The defensible finding is narrower than the candidate: *a coupon intended for anything else in the account will silently discount credit packs, because there is no product-scoping and no reconciliation.*\n\n---\n\n**`[nextjs/lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts:447]` REFUTED — recovery path exists; retry would not help either**\n\nThe claim's load-bearing premise is factually wrong:\n\n> \"it exists nowhere but the database — no email, no receipt, **no dashboard they can reach without the key**\"\n\nThere is one. `server/api/routers/customer.ts:30-78` exposes `licensesWithCredits` as a `protectedProcedure` that calls `getAccountKeysByEmail(userEmail)` (email from the Better Auth session) and returns `key: license.key`. Sign-in offers **magic link by email** (`src/lib/auth.ts:23` `magicLink({...})`; `app/[locale]/user/sign-in/page.tsx:93` `authClient.signIn.magicLink`). The buyer types the email they checked out with and sees the key. The Stripe `success_url` also lands them on `/purchase-success` whose CTA is `href=\"/user/dashboard\"` (`purchase-success/page.tsx:122`).\n\nSecond, the prescribed I5 remedy is inert here. Even if the route returned 500, the retry re-enters `handleCreditMint`, hits `grantResult === \"duplicate\"` at :402 and returns **before** the email — so a non-2xx buys nothing. And that early return is deliberate and load-bearing (:399-401):\n\n> `// Retry/duplicate delivery: credits already granted for this session — don't // re-send the email. (Pooling resolves the same license by email each retry, // so this guard is what keeps a re-delivery from emailing twice.)`\n\n**intentional:** duplicate-email suppression at :399-407, `git blame` `e3708d4`.\n\n---\n\n**`[nextjs/lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts:500]` REFUTED — the postulated transient state is not reachable**\n\n```ts\nconst sessions = await stripe.checkout.sessions.list({ payment_intent: paymentIntentId, limit: 1 });\nif (sessions.data.length === 0) { ...; return; }\n```\n\nThe claim rests on read-replica lag making a *just-refunded* charge's session invisible. But the Checkout Session is not new at refund time — it was created and completed days-to-weeks before the refund, and its `payment_intent` was indexed when the payment succeeded (which is precisely what let `checkout.session.completed` be processed in the first place). There is no fresh write for a replica to lag behind.\n\nThe realistic zero-result cases are all *definitive*: charges not originating from Checkout (invoices, dashboard/manual charges, direct PaymentIntents) — for which returning is correct, exactly as the sibling guard at :487-492 handles a missing `payment_intent`. If the `list` call itself fails transiently it **throws** and never reaches line 500; that failure mode belongs to `route.ts:125` below, not here.\n\n---\n\n**`[nextjs/lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts:517]` PLAUSIBLE — real unit mismatch, but the candidate's own scenario is arithmetically wrong**\n\nFirst, the load-bearing sub-claim checks out. `refundCreditGrant` (`src/lib/db-layer.ts:445-553`) does reclaim the **entire** grant regardless of the refunded fraction — it takes no amount parameter at all:\n\n```ts\nconst clawback = originalAmount - alreadyRefunded;   // db-layer.ts:478\n```\nand the call site passes only provenance (`:600-603`): `refundCreditGrant({ sourceType: \"stripe_credit_pack\", sourceId: checkoutSession.id })`. So crossing the :518 threshold by one cent destroys the whole pack.\n\nThe candidate's scenario is nonetheless **wrong**. $100 pack, `fee_cents: 600`, $50 fixed promo → `charge.amount = 5600`, `creditPortion = 5000`; a $50 refund returns *everything the buyer paid for credits* (the $6 fee being non-refundable by policy, `checkout/credits/route.ts:26-28`). Full clawback there is **correct**.\n\nThe genuine mismatch is with a **percentage** coupon, which Stripe prorates across both line items while `metadata.fee_cents` stays at its undiscounted value:\n- $100 pack, 50%-off → `charge.amount = 5300` (5000 credits + 300 fee actually paid). `creditPortion = 5300 - 600 = 4700`, understated by 300. A $47 refund — which does *not* return the $50 the buyer paid for credits — triggers a full 100,000-credit clawback. Harm is to the **customer**, ~`feeCents × discountFraction`.\n- The sharp edge: once the discount pushes `charge.amount` below the undiscounted `feeCents`, `creditPortion` goes **negative** and `charge.amount_refunded < creditPortion` is false for *every* possible refund. $500 pack (`fee_cents: 3000`, total 53000) at 95% off → `charge.amount = 2650`, `creditPortion = -350`. A $1 goodwill partial refund satisfies `100 >= -350` and wipes all 500,000 credits. There is no `Math.max(creditPortion, 0)` and no lower bound anywhere on the path.\n\nThe comment at :513-515 shows the author reasoned only about the undiscounted case (\"charge total minus the fee\") — `git blame` `9b53d84e`, the same commit that retired the license product. There are **no tests** on this file (`grep -rln \"stripe-webhook\\|handleChargeRefunded\"` matches only the route and the service).\n\n**Exact unproven link:** identical to :190 — whether an active *percentage* promotion code exists in the Stripe account. Also unverified from code: whether `managed_payments: { enabled: true }` (`checkout/credits/route.ts:195`) adds exclusive tax to `charge.amount`, which would inflate `creditPortion` and produce the *opposite* miss (a policy-compliant credit-only refund falling below the threshold and being silently skipped). Both need one dashboard read to settle.\n\n---\n\n**`[nextjs/lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts:593]` REFUTED — the guard is unreachable; its writer emits both keys atomically**\n\nThe observation that `creditAmount` is computed and then never consumed by the mutation is **correct** (`:600-603` passes only `sourceType`/`sourceId`). But the abort cannot fire. To reach :593 the session must already have passed `purchaseType === \"credits\"` at :512, and the sole writer of that key writes `credit_amount` in the same object literal (`checkout/credits/route.ts:79-83`):\n\n```ts\nconst metadata: Record<string, string> = {\n  purchase_type: \"credits\",\n  credit_amount: creditAmount.toString(),\n  fee_cents: feeCents.toString(),\n};\n```\n\nYou cannot have `purchase_type: \"credits\"` without `credit_amount`. And the value is always parseable and positive: `creditAmount = amount * 1000` with `amount ∈ [5, 500]` integers → `[5000, 500000]`. The finder's postulated sources (\"an older deploy, a Stripe Payment Link, dashboard-edited metadata\") are all out-of-repo operator actions, not code paths. This is dead defensive code — a tidiness nit (drop the unused `creditAmount` or the guard), not a lost-clawback bug.\n\n---\n\n**`[nextjs/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:125]` CONFIRMED (narrow) — the catch is broader than its own stated scope; a transient failure is silently retired**\n\nTrigger path: `POST /api/webhooks/stripe` (signature-verified `charge.refunded`) → `route.ts:121-124` → `handleChargeRefunded(charge, event.id)` → `stripe-webhook.ts:495` `await stripe.checkout.sessions.list({...})` throws on a Stripe 429/5xx or socket timeout → propagates to `route.ts:125` → logged → falls through to `return NextResponse.json({ received: true })` at `:132`. Stripe records the event as delivered and stops.\n\nThe intent comment is explicit and I am **not** disputing that the catch is deliberate:\n\n```ts\n} catch (error) {\n  console.error(\"Stripe webhook: Error processing refund:\", error);\n  // Don't return error status - log for manual review instead\n  // This prevents infinite retries for non-transient failures\n}\n```\n\nThat is the finding: the comment scopes the suppression to **non-transient** failures, and the implementation suppresses **all** of them. `handleChargeRefunded` performs a live third-party API call (`:495`) and three DB transactions (`refundCreditGrant` at `:553` and `:600`, `updateAccountKey` at `:565`) — every one of which has a genuinely transient failure mode that the author's stated rule says should retry. There is no error classification, no `err instanceof Stripe.errors.StripeConnectionError` branch, no dead-letter record. `git blame` `e3708d4`; no tests exist for this file.\n\nScope corrections to the candidate, stated plainly:\n- **Harm is bounded and non-corrupting**: a refund clawback is silently skipped and a human must notice from a log line. The refunded customer keeps their credits. There is no attacker lever and no double-mutation.\n- The candidate's second half — the `refundCreditGrant`(:553) / `updateAccountKey`(:565) split-application leaving credits clawed back but a license still `granted` — is **refuted for new purchases**: that branch requires `purchase_type === \"license\"` (:528), which no current code path can produce (see :133). It remains reachable only for a refund of a *legacy* license purchase, since `handleChargeRefunded` reads `purchase_type` from the live Stripe session rather than from repo state. Narrow, historical, and shrinking.\n\nMinimal fix consistent with the author's own intent: classify the error and re-throw (return 500) for connection/timeout/5xx/DB-serialization failures, keeping the 200-swallow for the genuinely non-transient cases the comment describes."}],"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1426,"cache_read_input_tokens":97375,"output_tokens":5728,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1426},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":5728,"cache_read_input_tokens":97375,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1426,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1426,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdWJP8Q8GDYgmGTZeiZkW","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"373f52e3-20c3-477a-ad52-0a95dcf23030","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:52:56.657Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}
